ParlaMint-DK_20210517160848	The debate is open and the first to speak is the Minister for Employment.
ParlaMint-DK_20210517160918	Thank you. With the motion for a resolution here, SF focuses on a key issue, namely how we can strengthen the organisation of the Danish labour market. The functionality and legitimacy of our well-functioning labour market model depends on a high degree of organisation, both on the employee side and on the employer side. It is therefore worrying that the organisation over a number of years has been declining, especially among traditional trade unions. They lift a great deal of social responsibility by concluding agreements broadly, by developing the field of training and by finding flexible solutions to accommodate the new forms of employment on the labour market, such as: in relation to those atypically employed and those who work via digital platforms. If we are to maintain a strong Danish model, it is important that there is a broad focus on the importance of employees and employers in all industries also in the future choosing to be a member of a trade union or an employers' association. It is therefore very important for the government that we strengthen the Danish model, but we must do so on an enlightened basis and in broad dialogue with among others. the social partners and experts in industrial relations. The Danish model contains the clear division of labour, that the social partners regulate pay and working conditions through agreements, and that the Folketing ensures that the social partners have the necessary framework to act within. The current rules on a tax credit of DKK 6,000. Every year for the union quota is an expression of a broad political desire to support the organisation of the Danish labour market and applies independently of the size of the quota and regardless of the tasks performed by each trade union. A debate about the importance of trade unions and increased organisation through tax deductions contains various aspects, but is basically about how we want to safeguard the Danish labour market model and also about how we develop it. In Denmark, Danish employees and employers can set up and join exactly the associations they want and also call them what they want. This also means that it is up to the individual trade union and its members themselves to determine what the union stands for and what tasks and what interest the association stands for. It is therefore up to the individual employee to decide with himself what one considers to be a trade union's core task and where one wants to be a member. This is the core of freedom of association in the Danish labour market. However, it is important to have the debate and remind each other of the role of professional organisations in our society and the tasks they undertake. It is at the heart of the Danish model that we have a high degree of organisation and that the majority of employees are covered by agreements. Agreements and large membership with the trade unions are the cornerstone of a strong and flexible labour market. It is a core task for a trade union to ensure proper pay and working conditions in the various disciplines. This is done by collective agreements and, where appropriate, by applying strikes and blockades in order to achieve that objective. And there is a difference between the so-called yellow trade unions and the traditional trade unions organized in the main organisations in FH and AC. The traditional trade union movement is based on professional affiliation and struggles to achieve broad wage and working conditions in the Danish labour market, but also to develop the labour market so that it is flexible and competitive. Among other things: Employment pensions and vocational training are examples of the traditional trade union movement carrying out tasks of social gravity. And the well-functioning shop steward system is an important element in a busy day-to-day business life where the shop steward is a key person in the day-to-day cooperation. Unlike traditional trade unions, the yellow organizations do not or to a limited extent use resources to obtain collective agreements in a professional field to the benefit of all employees, to fight to achieve collective agreements through strike or blockade, to develop and maintain a system of trust representatives and to develop and maintain the profession itself. It is appropriate to make it clear in the debate that there is a significant difference between the traditional trade unions and the yellow unions and the tasks they perform. And this must be borne in mind by the individual employee when choosing a trade union. The government agrees with the assessment of the challenges underlying the SF's motion for a resolution, which is why it is high priority for the government to strengthen the organisation of the Danish labour market. This applies both on the employee side and on the employer side. But we need to think carefully about what concrete measures will be appropriate. Here, adjustments to the rules on tax deductions can be one measure among several. In the first place, however, there is a need for a thorough analysis of the various aspects of strengthening the organisation. It also includes an analysis of the organisation on the employers' side and of the barriers to employees making a positive choice to be organized. Against this background, the government cannot support the resolution, but welcomes the debate. Thank you for the floor.

ParlaMint-DK_20210517172904	The debate is open. First of all, we must welcome the Minister for Employment. Here you go.
ParlaMint-DK_20210517172926	Thank you very much. And thank you for the opportunity to discuss the Danish holiday money system, not least, of course, for once again having the opportunity to praise the great work of the social partners in the Committee on Vacation Law when the new holiday law comes into being. A new law introducing a completely new holiday system with a contemporary holiday. A job in which employees and employers, through their representatives, came together and found solutions to difficult problems. Today we are going to deal with a resolution from the New Civil, Conservative and Liberal Alliance. The parties have jointly chosen to propose the abolition of the holiday money system, an issue which from time to time appears in different contexts. The authors of the proposal justify the proposal by saying that the Danes can work out how to save and manage their private economy. They therefore believe that it is paternal that the Danes cannot get their own money immediately, but must make use of a holiday money system. On the whole, I must start by stating that the purpose of the holiday law is to protect the safety and health of workers. The current vacation system ensures that employees pay for holidays and ensures that their employees have an interest in giving both head and body a well-deserved breath of air sometimes. This is very good for the working environment and helps prevent wear and tear. I fear that a system in which the employee receives his or her holiday allowance, together with his or her salary, could tempt employees to have a smaller holiday. This would serve neither the health of the individual employee nor society, as everything else would lead to a faster weardown of the workforce. Having said that, Denmark is also obliged to comply with the EU Working Time Directive, and these rules set limits on how much we can change the holiday law at national level. For example: employees are entitled to four weeks' annual paid leave under the EU Working Time Directive. The leave may not be replaced by a cash allowance unless the employment relationship ends. This means that it will not be in accordance with EU law that the holiday allowance is paid together with the salary, because this is precisely a significant risk that the paid holiday money will not be used to take a holiday. In this context, Denmark has also provided for the possibility that the fifth holiday week to which the Vacation Act entitles may be paid without the holiday being taken. The Vacation Act is to a large extent also part of the collective agreements and thus part of the balance between the two sides of industry. In other words, a change in the holiday law could upset the balance between the social partners. With this in place, we can also note that Denmark recently received a new holiday law, which entered into force on 1 September 2020; a holiday law, which the social partners are behind and which a united Parliament supported. As I said, the new holiday law introduced a vacation at the same time so that you can take the holiday immediately after you have earned the holiday. This is a huge step forward, especially for new people in the labour market, and for those who have temporarily left the labour market. The time when the employee earns vacation in one year and can only take the holiday in the following holiday year is over. The paid holiday money therefore comes much faster to pay than before when the holiday is taken. But now back to the proposal. For the government, it is important to maintain the system of income and a subsequent payment when the holiday is held, as it guarantees the employee the possibility of rest periods in the form of holidays. And yes, it is certainly the attitude of the government that holiday money should be in the pockets of employees. The employees just need them to keep a well-deserved rest in a busy everyday life; a rest where they should not worry about the economy. In conclusion, I would like to make it clear once again that the consequences of the proposal would be an infringement of EU law. The government therefore sees no reason to abolish the holiday money system. Thank you for the floor.

ParlaMint-DK_20210517182825	The debate is open and first of all we must welcome the Minister for Employment. Here you go.
ParlaMint-DK_20210517182851	Thank you very much for the floor. Today we are debating a motion for a resolution to abolish the offsetting of income from work in the basic amount of the national pension. The aim of the proposal is to reward the participation of the elderly in the labour market. The government would like to see even more seniors who can and will continue to work even if they have reached retirement age. But we in the government are also concerned that the individual will have a dignified end to working life. This means, in particular, that those who have had a long and hard working life will be entitled to an early retirement pension. I am pleased and proud that we have succeeded in guaranteeing this right to this group of seniors who, if any, have contributed to the Danish welfare society through a long working life. I would like to acknowledge that the Conservative People's Party, with its motion for a resolution, is focusing on the fact that as a society we must reward those who take an extra turn in our labour market. In this context, it may also be relevant to look at making it easier to understand the possibilities and benefits of working after the retirement age, which is why I am pleased that the retirement and wear-out commission is looking precisely at simplifying the rules of the pension system. The Commission is also looking at whether the pension system is designed so that it will also in future support seniors who can and want to stay longer in the labour market. It is a commission set up by the parties behind the senior pension agreement, a conciliation group, of which the Conservative People's Party is also a part. Against this background, of course, it is a little surprising that the Conservative People's Party is tabling this motion for a resolution and is not awaiting the report of the Commission which you yourself have helped to set in motion. I think it is wisest to await the outcome of the work of the Commission and I look forward to receiving the Commission's report in 2022. In recent years, the focus has been on maintaining seniors in the labour market. Social democracy has also helped to introduce a number of other measures in recent years which have made it economically more attractive to work after the retirement age. I believe that today we have good arrangements that reward those who are full-time or part-time and who continue to work after they reach their retirement age. You do not have to receive a retirement pension because you have reached the age of a national pension. Anyone who has reached the retirement age may choose to postpone the retirement pension and participate actively in the labour market for at least 750 hours a year, as opposed to later raising the retirement pension by a supplement, a waiting rate. The rate of waiting is increased, the longer the pension is suspended. People's pensioners with high labour income will benefit from the possibility of a retirement pension and thus avoid their income from work leading to an escalation of their oldage pension. Instead, they can obtain the pension set up at a time when, for example, they are entitled to a pension. have a lower or no income at all. In addition, greater flexibility has been introduced in 2018 in relation to how to get the waiting allowance paid, and it is precisely to make it more attractive to use the scheme, since for example, you can choose a faster payment of the waiting allowance. I can also point out that the basic allowance for the income of pensioners in the case of personal work has been increased several times, so it now amounts to DKK 122,004. a year. In addition, the reduction of the retirement pension on the basis of the income of a non-retired spouse or collector has been relaxed. This means that pensioners working next to the retirement pension have been given more money in their hands. I think that is a good thing. In particular, a tax-free senior premium has been introduced if you work at least 1,560 hours for one year after the age of the population. The prize is 43,697 DKK. for work in the first year after the retirement age and at DKK 26.010. for the second year after the retirement age. We can see that the scheme has got off to a good start since it was introduced in 2019. The first senior prizes have been paid from September 2020 and up to February 2021 the senior prize has been paid to a total of 5.545 persons. That's about 16%. of the employed persons who have had the age to be eligible for the senior premium. That so many citizens have received a cash reward for their efforts in the labour market, I think we can only be satisfied with. Today, the basic amount of the pension is reduced by 30%. of the income from personal work over DKK 344.600. ParlaMint-DK_20210517185346	The debate is open and the Minister for Employment is already ready, so go ahead with the Minister.
ParlaMint-DK_20210517185408	Thank you. Now I've been up here so many times, I kind of know the rhythm. I'm sorry if I anticipated the President's introduction. But thank you for the floor. With the motion for a resolution here, the Conservative People's Party proposes two amendments to the rules on the allowances for self-employed workers. The first proposal is to abolish the three-week waiting period before a self-employed person who ceases to work can receive a daily allowance and, in addition, it is proposed to abolish the one-week waiting period in the event of bankruptcy. The proposal is based on a desire that employees and self-employed persons should be treated equally in the benefit system. In addition, Conservatives propose that the self-employed person may choose to calculate his daily allowance rate on the basis of the two best financial years in the last 5 years. The proposal is justified by the fact that special consideration must be given to self-employed persons, because their income during the last few years before unemployment is generally not representative of their income. It also refers to the fact that self-employed persons have had unfavourable income conditions as a result of the closure of corona. To the authors of the motion for a resolution, I would like to start by saying that, in my opinion, these are changes to the rules that have been negotiated, and I am always prepared, as I shall say later, to discuss the rules and the consequences of them in the conciliation circle. I would like to start with the proposal to abolish the waiting period for self-employed people, and I would first like to state that I agree with the premise that employees and self-employed people should be treated equally in the benefit system as much as possible. It's only fair. At the same time, however, it is also important to emphasise that there are some differences between employees and self-employed people which make complete harmonisation not always possible. Today, as the motion for a resolution mentions, there is a waiting period before the self-employed can receive cash benefits. The waiting period is three weeks at regular end and one week at bankruptcy or foreclosure, and I would like to mention here that the waiting period for self-employed people is intended precisely to provide a level playing field for self-employed people and employees. When an employee becomes unemployed, he or she is subject to the rules on self-inflicted unemployment. This means, among other things, that if you quit your job as an employed employee for no valid reason or are responsible for your dismissal yourself, you will get a 3-week leave allowance for the first time. It is a quarantine which at the same time consumes the daily allowance period. If this occurs several times within a period of 12 months, entitlement to benefit shall lapse and he must satisfy an employment requirement in order to be able to receive benefit again. The rules on self-inflicted unemployment have been introduced to ensure that benefit recipients are available for the labour market and do not choose to be on unemployment benefit rather than in employment. This is a fundamental principle of the benefit system. Selfemployed persons are not covered by the rules on self-inflicted unemployment at the time of unemployment. The reason is that it may be difficult to determine whether unemployment, i.e. the closure of one's business or bankruptcy, is self-inflicted in relation to the self-employed. For an employee, an employer will be able to disclose the specific reason for termination or dismissal and the cashier will be able to assess whether the termination or dismissal is self-inflicted. The same paragraph does not apply to self-employed persons and therefore self-employed persons are not covered by the rules on self-inflicted unemployment. They used to be, but it was simply too difficult to administer. Selfemployed persons have the said waiting period if they become unemployed and apply for cash benefits. The waiting period, contrary to what applies to employees in self-inflicted unemployment, will not consume the benefit period. The former Left Government set up a working group in February 2016 to look at the complex cash benefits rules for self-employed people. The social partners were also involved here. An essential focus of the work was to make recommendations on how self-employed, fee recipients and freelancers could have the same conditions as employees. The Working Party recommended maintaining the existing rules on waiting periods for self-employed persons, which was added to the later agreement on a new benefit system for the future labour market. It was an agreement reached by the then VKLA government in May 2017 with the Social Democracy and the Danish People's Party. So, to sum up, the waiting period for self-employed people is intended to ensure a level playing field for self-employed people and employees in an area where the differences between the two groups make it difficult for them to be covered by exactly the same rules. The rules have been examined by a working group which recommended keeping the waiting period for self-employed people. I do not think that the proposal equates self-employed people and employees, since self-employed people are not covered by the rules on self-inflicted unemployment. That is why I am opposed to the proposal. Finally, I would like to say that I believe that a change in the rules should be discussed within the benefit board; I am always open to that. This brings me to the second proposal for an extended calculation period for self-employed people. The authors propose to change the basis for the calculation of the benefit rate for selfemployed persons. The aim is that selfemployed persons can always choose that the best two years within the last five years must constitute the basis for calculating the daily allowance rate. The current rate calculation rules are based on the fact that self-employed persons who have earned the right to unemployment benefit in the last three years preceding unemployment are calculated as the 12 months with the highest income out of the last 24 months. The rate calculation rules reflect a balance between the rate calculated on the basis of income over a longer period of time and the rate reflecting a person's relative current income. The method of calculating the rates is the same for wage earners, wage earners and self-employed workers, and it will be possible to combine the different types of income to calculate the rate. This entry and method of calculating rates is called step 1. However, selfemployed persons already have an additional opportunity to earn the right to benefit. If they do not have the possibility of earning the benefit rights of three years of combined income, they may earn on the basis of the income of the last five years from the self-employed enterprise. Here you can calculate the rate from the best 2 years out of the last 5 years of earning, and the entry and method of calculating the rate is called step 2. Step 2 was introduced precisely as a special possibility, because in some cases self-employed people may have lower incomes in the period leading to the closure of a company, in other words the very problem that the proposers want to deal with. The rules for calculating the rates for self-employed persons were introduced on the basis of recommendations from the previously mentioned Working Party on Self-employed Persons in the benefit system. Thus, it is already possible for the selfemployed to calculate the rate as the best two years out of the last five years of income if they have had low incomes over the last three years prior to unemployment and thus cannot earn the right to benefit in three years. Of course, I am always prepared to discuss the current rules and proposals for improvements, but I also have a little to hear from the proposers, on which basis they believe the current solution is not sufficient. I simply lack a more solid basis to say that the rules need to be changed when there is already an opportunity in the rules on daily allowances. Unfortunately, we do not yet have the basis for figures to make a full assessment of how the new rate rules for self-employed people work. On the preliminary basis of the figures, it is obvious that selfemployed people receive the current maximum benefit rate to a large extent and thus have a high average benefit rate calculated. However, it is the Ministry of Employment's assessment that the inventory is not made on an adequate data basis, and that is why it must of course be taken with reservations. When we have better data at a later stage, we will be able to analyse it further. So I will not vote in favour of the proposal to extend the rate-fixing rules in general for self-employed people. However, I would also like to say here that I am always willing to discuss proposals for changes and improvements in the conciliation circle. I propose that we do so when, with the evaluation of the rules for self-employed people, we have a better basis for assessing the consequences. It is my expectation that the evaluation will be ready by 2022. In relation to the economy, it is estimated by the Ministry of Employment that the two proposals together involve additional post-tax expenditure and a return of 30 million. kr. annual. Of course, we also miss the fact that the proposers have a concrete view of how it should be financed. In addition, I would like to point out that the motion for a resolution is about permanent changes to conciliation rules. In conclusion, I am fully aware that the covid-19 situation has created very special challenges for the self-employed and their ability to maintain an income by 2020. This may affect the rate calculation of self-employed persons. For this reason, the Government has also proposed that self-employed persons in step 1 may be allowed to extend the basis for calculating the benefit or early retirement pension to 36 months. I believe that this will alleviate the vast majority of the challenges of calculating the daily allowance rate for self-employed people in the context of the covid-19 situation. Thank you for the floor.

I could hear that the Minister has some figures, and we would like to have them verified. We will be voting yellow on the proposal here because it contains both elements that we can support, but also elements that we want to see clarified further. Finally, as several others have said, I would like to say that the Left also believes that we should ensure that the adjustments in the benefit system, as we are talking about here, contain elements of something that has been agreed in an agreement in 2017, when we entered into this agreement, take place in that circle, so that we secure broad political support. Thank you for the floor.

ParlaMint-DK_20210517191733	Thank you. There is no brief comment to be made on the rapporteur of the New Citizens' Party, but we have just one rapporteur that I missed, and that is Mrs Victoria Velasquez of the Enhedslisten. Here you go.
ParlaMint-DK_20210517191754	We must ensure stronger social rights for small businesses. We do not hear much about this in the debate that is often going on, but the issue of social security is really crucial for many entrepreneurs and for many self-employed people. For a lot of people, this also means that you never dare try to be independent or entrepreneurial. 70% of employees have said that it is the concern about the possibility of obtaining maternity leave or cash benefits if it becomes relevant that they would never dare to make the attempt as an independent or entrepreneur. Since that study was carried out, we in the Enhedslisten, together with the government and the understanding parties, have ensured better maternity rights for self-employed people, and during the corona crisis we have made some small steps in the area of benefits for self-employed people, but they have been temporary and not altered by the fundamental problem. We should change that, both because everyone deserves to be able to live with a high level of social security, and because the lack of security keeps too many from dared to take the leap, dared to start up as self-employed, even though they may have a thrilling idea and they will be able to put forward a sustainable business concept. For us in the Enhedslisten, it is important that we, as an entrepreneur or self-employed person, should, as far as possible, benefit from the same social security as employees. That is why we have also made a concrete demand for better rights for self-employed people in the benefit system, because frankly, if you contribute on an equal footing as employees to the benefit system, why is it that you do not have a right to equal treatment? Why isn't there this equality? In 2017, an agreement was made in the field of unemployment benefits which, on the one hand, brought improvements and, on the other hand, caused deterioration. There were improvements for those who are so-called combinators they are those who have both an income as an employee and as a self-employed person because now income as both self-employed and employee can be included in the calculation. But there were also deteriorations and backward steps in the agreement. Part of the financing of the agreement was that persons with a so-called self-employment received a reduced benefit period from 78 to 30 weeks and, at the same time, the minimum benefit rate for self-employed persons was removed and a calculation of the benefit rate based on earnings during the previous 24 months was introduced. This may therefore be problematic, because it simply means that some self-employed people are at risk of obtaining a very low rate of unemployment benefit, as they may experience a period of very low earnings before their business ends. I think that is quite logical, so that if a company ceases, it has probably had a period up to which you have had very low income. And this is a problem which has been widely recognised before, because we have just solved this last week, I think, with some of the aid packages in relation to the benefit system. So there we have, for example. dispensed with the rules and solved this problem here during the corona crisis. In addition, the 2017 agreement maintained a fundamental but problematic requirement, which I am very pleased that those behind the motion for a resolution are helping to highlight, and that is because it is the problematic requirement that one must completely shut down one's business in order to be entitled to benefit if one's business is one's main activity. Quite frankly, it is not appropriate, and it is actually quite short-term, to be forced to shut down one's business if an economic crisis hits society and, for a period of time, lowers profits. Because here you get pushed out to sell your inventory, sell it all, and it just makes you have even longer to get an opportunity to start up again. In the Enhedslisten, we do not want people who experience liquidity problems for a short period of time to shut down the company completely and dispose of everything, including their tools, everything that belongs to the work. Instead, we want the benefit system to create opportunities for you to have an income until you can get your business going again. And if you have a company, you should also be able to put it into hibernation, and we have concrete suggestions on how to do so. I would very much like to discuss this with the Conservatives. And my impression was, in fact, that the Minister for Business would also create that dialogue this spring. We're still waiting for that to happen. Thank you.
ParlaMint-DK_20210517192305	Thank you to Enhedslisten's rapporteur. There are no brief comments. Then we have reached the point where the rapporteur for the proposers will be put on the floor, and that is Mrs Mona Juul of the Conservative People's Party.
ParlaMint-DK_20210517192326	Thank you for that and thank you for the floor, and thank you for a good first reading with many positive expressions. Yes, this is gonna work. I've waited intriguingly for you as parties to stand up for yourselves. Should today really at long last be the day when we will have equal employees and self-employed workers in the field of unemployment benefit? Because, of course, there should not be a difference between the benefits available to self-employed people and those available to employees, should there? I think we're gonna change that. I'm listening that we can actually do that. The quite unreasonable so-called waiting period must go away and, of course, the self-employed should not have calculated future unemployment benefit on the basis of an average of the last 12 months with the most income in the last 24 months before unemployment. Because it is precisely in the time for you to have to close, that you may have to throw the towel, that the earnings get to the bottom. So it is simply not fair and it is not a reasonable basis for calculation. Even if there is a majority in favour of such a motion for a resolution as this one, there are still some things that we need to look at, and the rapporteur before me mentioned some of them. After all, companies must first be closed and shut down, everything must be sold, the CVR number must be closed and dismantled before you can raise your daily allowances as a self-employed person, which you have paid for yourself. We would also like to discuss how we can do better, but we just chose to take it a little bit in pieces, because it must obviously be a struggle to safeguard the independent basic rights. In particular, we have seen it under corona, i.e. the lack of focus on the self-employed, i.e. those who have to secure the jobs of today and the future, i.e. those who have the courage and the desire to bake the social cake bigger, so that the slices do not just get thinner and thinner and never get all the way around. When the Corona knocked, a tripartite agreement on wage compensation was quickly drawn up, but the fourth party was forgotten; the self-employed were forgotten. When the rate of wage compensation was later raised, it just happened not to include the self-employed, and when the rate of hard pressure was finally lifted for the self-employed, it came with a condition of how much they had to pay for themselves. Who do we think we are? That's what I think sometimes. Where do we really think the entrepreneurs of the future would rather start their business in a tax battle, a heavy bureaucratic system with little opportunity to raise capital, and where, at the same time, we do not support it if you roll over? This is sometimes done as an entrepreneur and self-employed person. It's inevitable. Ideas are constantly being developed to strengthen and support entrepreneurship, which is made a sea of ideas, but not always so much happens. The government does not even want to abolish the Phantom tax, which, in fact, does not cost anything and which will ensure that entrepreneurs and companies within, for example. Biotech will be able to attract capital and thus grow and create jobs. I very much fear that the many good intentions end in pool fun, where public funds are sent back to public bodies. There's nothing we can do with that. But with this resolution we will at least create some kind of justice, but we will also ensure that self-employed people are not just allowed to sail their own seas, because we know that this is one of the barriers to start up. Because we are not like the United States, where everyone is his own lucky blacksmith, but we are in Denmark, where we have a different mental attitude, where security also matters, where we need at least as much as many people throw themselves into running their own business, and we need to understand that those who want to be self-employed are whether you are a game developer, café owner, bikesmith or sell children's clothes online. They may do so for my sake, but we also need all the others, i.e. those who just earn the day and the way, and who may even contribute jobs or just want to work exactly like this. And the wild thing is that this costs an exceptionally small amount and that it will probably only contribute to the courage of more people to become self-employed and that this really amounts to additional revenue. ASE has helped us in Conservatives to calculate it, and we have come to the conclusion that it will cost a maximum of 15 million. kr. to drop the waiting period. With regard to the method of calculating rates, we have to ask the Minister through the committee, and the Minister has already mentioned an amount here. We should try to see if we can count on it together. But overall, it can hardly be the large amount in a social perspective, in that self-employed people make up so few of the total number of annual unemployment cycles and, moreover, self-employed people draw on the benefit system for less time than employees. There is no one in this country who wants to be on unemployment benefits, but there is certainly no self-employed person who does. At least I don't know them. Conservatives have set aside 300 million. kr. in reserve in our own financial act, which we could easily use exactly for this, which is a relatively small amount of money that needs to be raised, and I am sure that if the government could just do a little magic, then we could also find the funds if we wanted to. I hope and look forward to a good committee work that we may have a report text prepared and that we will meet in the conciliation circle so that we can once and for all secure the Danish self-employed and get done and do it a little more straight in the benefit system. Thank you very much for a good debate.
